‘Love Is Blind’ Creator on Protecting the Cast and Why He Chose to Keep Rob’s Attack on Production in the Final Cut

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: This post contains spoilers for Season 11 of “Love Is Blind,” including the reunion, now streaming on Netflix.

Another season of “Love Is Blind” has wrapped up. The Season 11 reunion dropped on Netflix on Sunday, revealing that the one couple who did say “I do” on their wedding day, Sarah Williams and John Shannon, called it quits after he was caught texting with an escort.

However, the most dramatic part of the season centered on Rob Feeley and Michaella Giorgio, a couple whose relationship exploded in the weeks leading up to their wedding. The messiness began when the pair went out with his friends; after one of the belligerent boys got cut off by the bartender, Feeley then broke the fourth wall and yelled at production to “cut the cameras,” saying, “You’re cut off, I’m cut off.” He started yelling, “I’m not this phony ass Hollywood bullshit.”

“Love Is Blind” creator and CEO of Kinetic Content, Chris Coelen, breaks down the scene below and explains why he decided to keep it in the show.

“The participants are not under our control. Some shows are produced in what they call a bubble, where the participants are really in a specific location, and they’re with you 24 hours a day. That is not the case on this particular show. So when we are with them, which is not all the time, obviously we look out for them like we would for anyone, and we document their choices,” explains Coelen. “They’re adults, and they’re making their own choices, and we’re there to follow that. I think what you see pretty clearly in that scene is that the bar doesn’t want to serve Rob’s friend anymore; that’s a choice that the bar made, which I wholly agree with.”

He adds that there’s obviously a great deal of editing in the show, since you’re putting days of content into one episode, “but I think we’ve always made the commitment to show the essence of their journeys in an accurate way, and I think that certainly showed who Rob was in that moment in a very accurate way.”

After the episode aired, many viewers online feared for Giorgio’s safety, something she tells Variety was never a concern.

“There was never a moment during filming or production where I felt physically unsafe. I also feel very secure in the fact that if I did, I could always speak with production about it,” Giorgio says. “I’m not shy about the things that I need or standing up for myself, so my choices to leave with Rob were 100% mine, and there was never a moment where I felt like he would be physical with me.”

In fact, after the bar, the group stayed out until the middle of the night. So the next day, it was time for the St. Patrick’s Day pod squad mixer, which only made matters worse; Feeley had a “crash out” (his words), yelling that the whole experience was fake, claiming he was going to be edited poorly and that no one there really cared about him. It ultimately led Giorgio to call off their engagement.

“Our obligation is always just to support them in whatever they believe. Nobody is ever required to participate in the experience. They are welcome, and we see it happen every season, all the time — people drop out,” says Coelen. “Rob saying, ‘Oh, this is Hollywood bullshit,’ well, it’s his choice. The next day, Michaella was saying, ‘I don’t want to go to this group event.’ They don’t have to go. Nobody’s forcing them to go to the group event. It’s his choice to participate. Our approach is to try to provide the scaffolding for the experience, and then they navigate through that. That’s what’s fascinating to me and what makes every single season unique. Everyone navigates through that in their own particular way.”

Seasons 10 and 11 only featured one couple getting married, and, as noted, this season’s didn’t last. However, Giorgio says at the reunion that she still feels like the experiment worked because she did find love. Coelen agrees.

“It doesn’t worry me at all. The success of a particular season is not based on how many people end up getting married or staying married or getting engaged,” he says. “They get engaged ostensibly because they’re in love. That’s extraordinary. But that’s not where it stops. The true test of ‘Love Is Blind’ is with the love that they formed: Is that capable of surviving the real world?”

“What I’m really proud of is, we don’t take a position on any of that. Our job is to be a reflection of these particular people’s journeys, and in doing that, I think we’re a really interesting mirror into the society as a whole,” says Coelen. “Going back to Season 1, and moving forward, you can really feel some of the currents in our culture in terms of the things that matter to the participants in making the biggest choice in their life.”

Strands, the New York Times' elevated word-search game, requires the player to perform a twist on the classic word search. Words can be made from linked letters — up, down, left, right, or diagonal, but words can also change direction, resulting in quirky shapes and patterns. Every single letter in the grid will be part of an answer. There's always a theme linking every solution, along with the "spangram," a special, word or phrase that sums up that day's theme, and spans the entire grid horizontally or vertically.

By providing an opaque hint and not providing the word list, Strands creates a brain-teasing game that takes a little longer to play than its other games, like Wordle and Connections.

NYT Connections hints today: Clues, answers for November 9, 2026

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The NYT Connections puzzle today is not too difficult if you talk a good game.

Connections is the one of the most popular New York Times word games that's captured the public's attention. The game is all about finding the "common threads between words." And just like Wordle, Connections resets after midnight and each new set of words gets trickier and trickier—so we've served up some hints and tips to get you over the hurdle.

If you just want to be told today's puzzle, you can jump to the end of this article for today's Connections solution. But if you'd rather solve it yourself, keep reading for some clues, tips, and strategies to assist you.

The NYT's latest daily word game has become a social media hit. The Times credits associate puzzle editor Wyna Liu with helping to create the new word game and bringing it to the publications' Games section. Connections can be played on both web browsers and mobile devices and require players to group four words that share something in common.

Each puzzle features 16 words and each grouping of words is split into four categories. These sets could comprise of anything from book titles, software, country names, etc. Even though multiple words will seem like they fit together, there's only one correct answer.

If a player gets all four words in a set correct, those words are removed from the board. Guess wrong and it counts as a mistake—players get up to four mistakes until the game ends.

Players can also rearrange and shuffle the board to make spotting connections easier. Additionally, each group is color-coded with yellow being the easiest, followed by green, blue, and purple. Like Wordle, you can share the results with your friends on social media.

Nvidia launches independent investigation into CEO after reports of suspected Chinese spy link

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Nvidia has launched an independent investigation into reports that CEO Jensen Huang was targeted by a foreign intelligence operation.

The company’s board announced the investigation on November 8, shortly after Huang admitted to employees that he had been unfaithful to his wife. Huang has not been removed as CEO and has pledged to cooperate with the review.

The investigation follows reports about Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, a former executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore-based Nvidia customer. US intelligence officials reportedly believed she was passing information to Beijing. The two Huangs are not related.

Breaking news from @ianmking: Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang admits to extramarital affair and the company board is now investigating him being targeted by a Chinese espionage plot. He remains CEO for now. https://t.co/jGYStYFo0M

Nvidia said its investigation will be overseen by independent directors and outside legal counsel. The board said it has “no reason to believe” that national security or Nvidia’s company secrets were compromised, while adding that it would review all available information.

The board also said Huang supports the investigation and remains in charge of the company.

In a message to Nvidia employees, Huang wrote that he had been unfaithful to his wife and that his actions had hurt her and their children. He said he took full responsibility, apologized to his colleagues and shareholders, and remained committed to leading Nvidia.

“Regarding the national security concerns raised in news reports,” Huang wrote, “Nvidia’s Board of Directors has initiated an independent investigation.” He also asked for privacy for his family.

The allegations first emerged in an October New York Times report claiming US intelligence agencies had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset. Megaspeed has purchased more than $4.8 billion worth of Nvidia chips over the past two years, according to the report. The Financial Times later reported that a US intelligence memo alleged Jensen Huang and Alice Huang had a romantic relationship.

Nvidia previously described Huang’s interactions with Alice Huang as ordinary business contact. Huang also told Bloomberg that Nvidia had examined Megaspeed’s GPU deployments and found no evidence that its chips had been illegally diverted to China.

China’s Foreign Ministry called the allegations “unsubstantiated” and described them as smears. Singapore’s Ministry of Home Affairs also said its officials had not claimed Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset and had not investigated her personally, although authorities confirmed that investigations into Megaspeed were ongoing.

Three House committee chairs have requested a closed-door briefing with Huang on November 18. They want answers about his interactions with Alice Huang and Nvidia’s security procedures for preventing foreign espionage.

The lawmakers asked Huang to accept the date or propose an alternative by November 11. There is currently no indication that Huang plans to step down while Nvidia’s internal investigation continues.

‘You Can See Everything’ Sweeps Critics Choice Documentary Awards

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

“You Can See Everything,” Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim‘s phenomenally successful documentary about disgraced Theranos founder Elizabeth Holmes, swept the Critics Choice Association‘s documentary awards tonight, winning each of its five categories — including Best Documentary Feature. Ava DuVernay’s “14th,” which headed into the night with the most nominations (six), went home empty-handed.

In addition to the top prize, “You Can See Everything” won Best Director (Fielder and Oppenheimer), Best Cinematography (David Bolen), Best Editing (Adam Locke-Norton), and Best Music Score (Ari Balouzian).

Whether or not this is a harbinger of the film‘s Oscar chances is anybody’s guess. A24 is aggressively campaigning for “You Can See Everything” to score both Best Documentary and Best Picture nominations. If the film receives the latter, it would be the first documentary ever to do so. The CCA sweep certainly doesn’t hurt its prospects, but it’s not necessarily a predictor either — in the last 10 years, the CCA and Academy have only agreed on the best documentary twice (with “O.J.: Made in America” and “Summer of Soul”). Other winners tonight included David Greaves’ “Once Upon a Time in Harlem,” which took home awards for Best First Documentary Feature, Best Historical Documentary and Best Archival Documentary, and Alex Gibney’s four-hour “Musk,” which won Best Biographical Documentary. John Wilson scored an award for Best Narration for his work as writer and performer on “A History of Concrete,” and Baz Luhrmann’s maximalist “EPiC: Elvis Presley in Concert” won Best Music Documentary.

The movie currently favored by many awards season pundits to take home the Best Documentary Oscar, “Naza,” won Best Political Documentary, and Best Science/Nature Documentary went to “Nuisance Bear.” Elizabeth Chai Vasarhelyi and Jimmy Chin’s widely acclaimed “Everest: The Other Side” was awarded Best Sports Documentary.

Then add political uncertainty, which is adding another headache. Democrats secured control of both congressional chambers during the US midterm elections. Investors now fear greater opposition to data center expansion and potentially tougher regulatory scrutiny.

The resulting selloff has exposed how closely crypto trades with speculative technology investments. Bitcoin increasingly moves alongside high-growth equities when institutional investors adjust their risk exposure.

The damage has also spread into derivatives markets. More than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions disappeared during Thursday’s selloff. Most liquidations involved traders betting on higher prices.

That forced selling can quickly turn an ordinary correction into something considerably uglier. As Bitcoin falls, liquidations push prices lower and expose leveraged altcoin positions to further losses.

Nvidia’s problems have suddenly become considerably more personal. The company’s board announced an independent investigation yesterday. It wants to examine reports that Huang was targeted by a foreign intelligence operation.

The controversy involves Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed. US authorities reportedly suspected her of being a Chinese intelligence asset.

Megaspeed has also faced scrutiny over allegations involving access to advanced Nvidia chips restricted from export to China. Republican lawmakers have requested a briefing from Huang regarding the allegations.

Separately, Huang admitted to being unfaithful to his wife. He apologized to his family and colleagues, accepting responsibility for his actions.

The combination creates an uncomfortable situation for Nvidia shareholders. Huang is not simply another corporate executive. He remains the public face of the AI revolution and one of its most influential decision-makers.

A prolonged investigation could introduce uncertainty around Nvidia’s leadership, relationships with international customers, and exposure to geopolitical tensions.

Nvidia’s board says it has no reason to believe company secrets or national security were compromised. However, the investigation could keep the controversy alive while investors are already questioning AI valuations.

The biggest concern for crypto is what happens if the AI selloff spreads. Nvidia has become a central pillar of the global technology investment cycle. Its chips power the infrastructure behind many of the world’s largest AI projects.

If investors begin questioning the sustainability of that investment cycle, semiconductor stocks, data center operators, and technology companies could face another round of selling.

Bitcoin is particularly exposed because institutional investors increasingly treat it as part of their risk portfolios. When confidence disappears from technology markets, crypto often loses buyers alongside speculative equities.

The Binance security incident has added another source of pressure. The exchange’s clients withdrew approximately $6.1 billion following the exchange’s recent security breach.

So why is crypto down? The AI market has a new problem to deal with, and Bitcoin is discovering that Nvidia’s troubles can become its troubles too.

What is Zcash (ZEC)? How shielded transactions work

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Learn about how privacy-focused cryptocurrency Zcash (ZEC) uses zero-knowledge proofs to protect transaction details.

Zcash (ZEC) is a privacy coin and blockchain launched in 2016. It allows users to make both public and private transactions. The latter — called “shielded transactions” on the network — make use of zero-knowledge proofs called zk-SNARKs to verify payments without revealing the sender, recipient or amount.

Zcash has emerged as one of the largest cryptocurrencies by market capitalization following a sharp rally in 2026, as growing institutional interest and renewed demand for financial privacy have brought the decade-old project back into the spotlight.

Zcash is a decentralized cryptocurrency designed to enable private digital payments. The Zcash network was originally derived from Bitcoin’s code base, beginning as an academic experiment in improving that network’s privacy protections. The network therefore retains some similarities to Bitcoin, including its use of proof-of-work mining to secure the network and fixed supply of 21 million coins.

The researchers behind Zcash originally conceived of a privacy extension for Bitcoin called Zerocoin. This idea developed into a separate protocol, Zerocash, which in turn provided the foundation for Zcash.

The main difference between Zcash and Bitcoin is their approaches to privacy. All activity on the Bitcoin network is public by design; anyone can view the balances of every address, along with their full transaction history, even though the identities of the holders are unknown.

Zcash, on the other hand, gives users the option to process their transactions privately. It offers transparent transactions, which function like a normal BTC transaction, as well as shielded transactions, which allow users to keep transaction details private through advanced cryptography.

The network launched in October 2016, and the nonprofit Zcash Foundation was established the following year to support the network and its development. Zcash has since evolved into a decentralized ecosystem spanning multiple independent organizations and contributors. The Zcash Foundation continues to maintain core infrastructure and support protocol development.

Zcash’s shielded transactions make use of cryptographic proofs to verify transactions are legitimate while keeping the underlying information concealed. This means that the transaction amount, sender’s address and recipient’s address are all kept private.

To understand how this works, it helps to look at how Zcash handles private funds. Transactions on the network are processed in two parallel but separate accounting environments: the transparent ledger and shielded pools. The latter is where private transactions take place; shielded pools are parts of the chain where funds can be held and transferred without their ownership, balance and history being publicly visible.

According to figures displayed on the official Zcash network dashboard, approximately 29% of the issued ZEC supply was held in shielded pools as of October 2026. It is also worth noting that transactions moving between transparent and shielded pools can reveal the amounts entering or leaving those pools, which in turn could be used to infer relationships between addresses.

While the regular blockchain uses conventional public addresses, shielded pools instead represent funds as cryptographic records called notes. When a user processes a shielded payment, their wallet uses existing notes to create new ones for the recipient. This transaction is recorded on the Zcash blockchain, but observers cannot see who received the notes, which notes were spent or how much ZEC changed hands.

The network stores public commitments to these notes, allowing it to keep track of balances without all of the transaction details displayed on a public ledger. Verifying that private transactions follow the rules of the blockchain was one of the original challenges of privacy coins, one which Zcash solves through a cryptographic mechanism called zero-knowledge proofs.

A zero-knowledge proof (ZKP) is a cryptographic method that allows someone to prove a statement is true without revealing the underlying information used to establish it. Imagine if someone wanted to prove they were over 18 without revealing their exact date of birth — ZKPs are able to achieve a similar effect by using cryptographic mathematics.

Instead of broadcasting all of the information needed to verify a payment, the sender generates a cryptographic proof of its validity. Other users can view and verify that proof — the product of the cryptographic algorithm — while the information used to generate it is kept concealed.

Zcash uses a specific type of proof called a zero-knowledge succinct non-interactive argument of knowledge, or zk-SNARK. It also makes use of cryptographic identifiers called nullifiers attached to notes to prevent double-spending.

Viewing keys introduce another level of control for Zcash users. These allow them to selectively reveal transaction data with third parties while keeping it hidden from the general public. These viewing keys give limited read-only access to the wallet without giving the holder the capability to spend the balance inside.

The network allows for different levels of visibility. For example, an incoming viewing key reveals only received payments, while a full viewing key reveals broader details of an account’s shielded activity.

This allows Zcash users to straddle the line between privacy and compliance. For example, an individual may need to reveal transaction records to their local tax authority during an audit, or a company may need to reveal its Zcash holdings to shareholders.

This is known as selective disclosure, allowing users to choose when and with whom they share financial information. Custom controls like this distinguish Zcash from networks like Monero (XMR), where all activity is anonymous by default.

Zcash supports transparent addresses that operate similarly to Bitcoin addresses and shielded addresses designed to protect transaction details. Unified addresses simplify the process by allowing users to manage both types of transaction from inside one wallet.

Sender, recipient and transaction amounts are publicly visible

zs1 for Sapling; newer shielded receivers are incorporated into unified addresses

Conceals transaction details when used in fully shielded transfers

Can support multiple receiving methods; privacy depends on which receiver is used

Zcash's privacy technology has undergone several major upgrades since the network launched in 2016. One of the biggest challenges facing its early developers was the need for a trusted setup, a process used to generate the cryptographic parameters required for its zero-knowledge proofs to function. This required multiple participants and left the network theoretically vulnerable to counterfeit shielded balances.

This convoluted process was streamlined by successive network upgrades:

The upgrade also restricted activity in the original Orchard pool and introduced a mechanism known as a turnstile, allowing existing funds to migrate to Ironwood while enforcing supply-integrity checks. Compatible wallets can handle this migration without requiring users to create a new wallet.

New ZEC coins enter circulation through proof-of-work mining. This is the same system used by Bitcoin, wherein miners dedicate computing power to solving complex mathematical problems, with the successful miners gaining the right to produce new blocks that are in turn validated by other nodes. The specific algorithm used by Zcash is called Equihash.

ZEC also shares a similar emission schedule with BTC, with a fixed supply cap of 21 million coins and a halving in new emissions roughly every four years. The most recent Zcash halving occurred in November 2024, cutting the block subsidy (the network reward paid to miners) to 1.5625 ZEC.

Unlike Bitcoin, however, Zcash also directs a portion of newly minted coins toward network development.  A new funding model was introduced in November 2025 which now sees 80% of block subsidies paid to miners, 8% to community grants and 12% to a development fund controlled by ZEC holders.

Zcash and Monero are both privacy-focused cryptocurrencies, but they take different approaches to transaction confidentiality. The key difference is that Zcash allows users to opt in or out of private transactions, whereas all transactions on Monero are private by default. Both networks support viewing keys to reveal transaction information to selected individuals.

Ring signatures, stealth addresses, RingCT

Not supported as a standard transaction mode

It’s important to note that neither ZEC nor XMR guarantee complete anonymity. Zcash balances can be tracked while moving in or out of private pools, while wallets on both networks can potentially be de-anonymized if they interact with a user’s centralized exchange accounts.

Which blockchain provides better privacy solutions depends on the circumstances. Generally speaking, Monero's default privacy removes the need for users to manage custom privacy settings inside their wallet, while Zcash's optional approach offers greater flexibility for compliance.

The regulatory footing of privacy coins is complex and differs between jurisdictions. Generally, ZEC is legal to own and use; however, restrictions can apply to exchanges and other crypto platforms that handle ZEC even where individual ownership is permitted.

The European Union has taken one of the clearest regulatory stances against privacy coins, introducing legislation prohibiting crypto service providers from maintaining anonymous blockchain accounts.

In response to these pressures and ambiguities, some cryptocurrency exchanges have delisted privacy coins due to compliance concerns. Others restrict users from using shielded pools for deposits and withdrawals, requiring that these go through standard transparent transaction channels instead.

Although this does not establish a blanket ban on individuals owning Zcash, measures such as these can affect the accessibility of the privacy coin sector. Users should verify the rules applying to their jurisdiction and chosen service provider before trading or transferring ZEC.

Zcash offers solutions for blockchain users concerned about privacy, but it also carries certain risks that holders should be aware of. These are technical, regulatory and financial.

No. Shielded transactions can conceal the sender, recipient and amount, but Zcash does not guarantee complete anonymity. Transparent transactions remain publicly visible, while exchange records, network information and user behavior can reveal identifying details.

2. What is the difference between shielded and transparent Zcash transactions?

Transparent transactions publicly reveal wallet addresses and transaction amounts, much like Bitcoin. Shielded transactions use zero-knowledge proofs to conceal this information while allowing the network to verify the payment. Transfers between transparent and shielded pools can still reveal the amounts entering or leaving those pools.

No. Zcash is a privacy blockchain and coin, while Tornado Cash is a mixer protocol. Privacy coins are distinct from cryptocurrency mixers, which attempt to obscure the origins and destinations of funds moving across otherwise transparent blockchains. They do this by automating a complex web of transactions to obfuscate the trail of funds.

Zcash’s optional privacy features are its core differentiator from Bitcoin. Although ZEC was originally derived from Bitcoin’s code base, its developers added shielded transaction pools that use zero-knowledge proofs to enable private transfers.

5. Is Zcash better than Monero for privacy?

Neither cryptocurrency is universally better for privacy. Monero provides transaction confidentiality by default, while Zcash allows users to choose between shielded and transparent transactions. Both have different privacy trade-offs, and neither guarantees complete anonymity.

Zcash ownership and use are generally permitted in many jurisdictions, although restrictions vary by country. Some jurisdictions, such as the EU, have introduced legislation restricting cryptocurrency platforms from maintaining anonymous accounts. Although not a blanket ban, this could limit the accessibility of ZEC when it takes effect in July 2027.

Zcash has a maximum supply of 21 million ZEC, matching BTC’s supply cap. New coins are issued through proof-of-work mining, with the block subsidy periodically reduced through halvings. The latest halving occurred in November 2024.

An editor checks the facts, figures and quotes they produce against primary sources, and every piece is reviewed by an editor before it is published. Sponsored content and press releases are labeled as such and are not written by our editorial team.

‘You Can See Everything’ Sweeps Critics Choice Documentary Awards

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Ron Howard and Jehane Noujaim were honored at the 11th annual celebration of nonfiction filmmaking in New York.

You Can See Everything won everything it was nominated for at the 2026 Critics Choice Documentary Awards.

The buzzy Elizabeth Holmes doc, directed by Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim, won all five awards for which it was nominated at Sunday’s awards. The sweep marks the latest triumph for the A24 film and the first major award wins for the project, which debuted a little over two months ago with a surprise Telluride screening. It then received a high-profile screening at the New York Film Festival as anticipation grew for the documentary ahead of its Oct. 16 theatrical release. Related Stories TV Druski Embodies Elizabeth Holmes in 'You Can See Everything' Spoof on 'SNL' Movies 'Godzilla Minus Zero' Barrels Toward Historic $32M Box Office Launch With 'Cat in the Hat' at No. 2

Since hitting theaters, You Can See Everything has grossed nearly $80 million at the domestic box office, making it one of the highest-grossing docs ever, trailing Fahrenheit 9/11 and Everest domestically. Worldwide, You Can See Everything is a little further down the list of all-time box-office performers, but the movie still hasn’t opened in many overseas territories.

In addition to a best documentary feature Oscar nod, it’s been speculated that You Can See Everything could land best picture and supporting actress nominations.

At the Critics Choice Documentary Awards, in addition to winning best documentary feature, You Can See Everything won best director (Fielder and Oppenheim), best cinematography (David Bolen), best editing (Adam Locke-Norton) and best score (Ari Balouzian).

Fellow five-time nominee Once Upon a Time in Harlem also won multiple awards, three, while Ava DuVernay’s 14th, which went into the awards with a leading six nominations, was shut out.

Nuisance Bear, which like You Can See Everything and Once Upon a Time in Harlem was nominated for five awards, won best science/nature documentary.

Other notable winners include four-time nominees John Wilson’s The History of Concrete, Everest: The Other Side, EPIC: Elvis Presley in Concert and Lion as well as triple nominees Musk, Alex Gibney’s four-hour Elon Musk examination, which won best biographical drama, and controversial Israel-Gaza war doc, NAZA, which won best political drama.

Elsewhere at the 11th annual Critics Choice Documentary Awards, hosted by Ross Mathews from New York’s Edison Ballroom, Ron Howard received the Critics Choice Impact Award and Jehane Noujaim was recognized with lifetime achievement honor the Pennebaker Award.

“This year’s winners reflect the extraordinary creativity, ambition and innovation shaping documentary filmmaking today,” Critics Choice Association vp documentary Christopher Campbell said in a statement. “From bold new storytelling approaches that have expanded the reach of nonfiction films and brought new audiences to the genre, to filmmakers who tackle pressing political and social issues, to those who return to compelling stories to explore them in greater depth, this year’s nominees demonstrate the limitless possibilities of the documentary form. We are thrilled to celebrate the remarkable talent and diverse perspectives that continue to shape the exciting future of documentary filmmaking.”

Report: MRI shows Patrick Mahomes avoided serious injury

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes underwent an MRI on his right elbow following Sunday’s 35-12 win over the Jets. The test did not reveal a major injury, Ian Rapoport of NFL Media reports.

The team will monitor Mahomes’ strained elbow over the next few days for pain and swelling.

“If I’m able to play, I’m going to play,” Mahomes said, via Nate Taylor of ESPN. “That’s part of the game. It scares you in moments, when you get some pains in certain areas of your body. Obviously, it didn’t feel great, but I was able to kind of get it worked on a little bit, especially at halftime.”

With 1:33 left in the second quarter, Jets edge rusher David Bailey hit Mahomes’ right arm on a strip-sack. Will McDonald IV and Darrell Jackson Jr. also hit Mahomes on the play.

Mahomes did not miss any snaps despite the injury.

Julie Frymyer, an assistant athletic trainer for the team, spent halftime helping Mahomes get his right arm loose. Frymyer was also instrumental in Mahomes’ rehab of his left knee after the quarterback underwent major surgery on Dec. 15.

“Any time it’s elbows and knees, it’s always scary, just because you just don’t know,” Mahomes said. “Even my knee last year, after we were doing the initial test, I thought, because I could walk off the field, I was going to be good. Then, all of a sudden, you do one test, and you’re just out [for the rest of the season]. That stuff scares you. Once I could throw the ball, I knew it wasn’t a ligament, and it was more muscles.”

It’s fire in Naples!

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

In Naples, the crisis is now more visible than ever. Traveling to Porto on Wednesday in the Champions League, the Azzurri conceded their seventh defeat of the season in all competitions (4-1), but also displayed worrying nervousness. The image of the heated altercation between Kevin De Bruyne and Lorenzo Lucca at the end of the game illustrated the tensions currently running through the Neapolitan locker room. In the process, Scott McTominay did not hide his annoyance at the situation. “ We have to play better in both areas, we can’t continue like this. We lost 4-1, obviously I’m angry. We have a lot of injuries, but that can’t be an excuse, even if it’s always the same players who come on the pitch. Everyone needs to go home and think about what they can do to improve tomorrow “, explained the Scottish community.

An annoyance which is justified by the worrying sporting situation of the Neapolitan club. Naples is in eighth place in Serie A, is already 14 points behind the leader, Lazio, and is nine points from fourth qualifying place for the C1. On the European scene, Massimiliano Allegri’s squad is the only Italian team not to appear among the top 24. Further proof of the division within the locker room, the exit of Matteo Politano from Sky Italiacriticizing foreign players in particular for not speaking out about the situation. “ Everyone needs to examine their conscience. To play matches that require such an expenditure of energy, you have to be able to make changes. Otherwise, we can’t do it, it’s simple. You can only maintain such a rhythm if you have eight or nine changes “. To try to escape this negative spiral, Naples is preparing to carry out a ritiro, according to La Repubblica.

A decision which would have been taken even before the new rout on the Dragão pitch, but which Allegri’s group seems to greatly need. The players should therefore remain together until the reception of Lazio (Sunday, 12:30 p.m.), with the objective of closing ranks and preparing as best as possible for the arrival of the championship leader. Facing the Romans, Massimiliano Allegri himself spoke of a “ final », as the urgency is felt. While the European markets are moving dangerously apart and internal tensions are accumulating, the Italian technician is more threatened than ever. In the event of another defeat, he could leave Napoli, only four months after his arrival.

Why 17th-Century Parisian Streetlights Were The OG Flock Cameras

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

For the past couple years, Flock Safety and its vast network of spy cameras have been all over the news, and for numerous reasons. Whether you've heard about them because of the staggering number of Flock cameras dotting US highways, because of the CEO calling Flock detractors terrorists, or the massive number of cops that keep getting arrested for illegally using automatic license plate readers (ALPRS) to stalk people, one thing's for sure: You've definitely seen news about this state-sponsored private surveillance network (and its similarly dubiously-intentioned competitors, like Axon) for one reason or another. But you might be surprised to learn that you can trace a line back through history from modern surveillance tech like ALPRs back to the some of the first streetlights, which appeared in Paris in the late 1600s.

Like ALPRs, these public lights weren't well-loved by the public, especially by revolutionaries and the lower classes, who were particularly affected by the policing the lights enabled. The similarities don't end there, though. Just like people today love vandalizing Flock cameras and other ALPRs, the Parisians of the 17th and 18th centuries loved smashing the lanterns lighting up the city streets. Like today, such vandalism carried significant legal ramifications, but people still saw fit to express their discontent by destroying the lights, a behavior that only became more popular with the beginning of the French Revolution.

It seems like the French have always been creative and prolific protesters, and the ongoing 2026 French student protests we're seeing today are just a continuation of that legacy. So maybe it should come as no surprise that the French showed us how to push back against state and private surveillance alike more than 300 years ago.

Today, streetlights are abundant, and they've become a completely normalized part of society. You'll find them all over in urban areas, and they line many sections of highway, as well. They're so prevalent that you might only notice their omnipresent status when one goes out in the city, or on a section of road that's suddenly surprisingly dark. With them being such a mainstay of our everyday lives, it may seem difficult to imagine streetlights as surveillance technology, but that is exactly what they were used for when they first started cropping up in European cities a few hundred years ago.

The first street lights in Paris were installed during the reign of King Louis XIV while the night life of aristocrats was booming, but murder rates in the city meant partying all night wasn't safe for the wealthy. The King appointed a head of police who turned to public lighting to solve the issue, which kept aristocrats safe while also enabling increased scrutiny on poor people and vagrants.

While the lamps were initially intended to curtail murder rates, the ruling class quickly realized it could use them to police activities of all types, and not just crimes. The technology enabled the French government to crackdown on vagrancy, petty crime, and even political dissidence on a level never seen before.

If that sounds familiar, it's because ALPRs are enabling similar — but even more wide-reaching — policing behaviors today. Except ALPR technology isn't that accurate and its warrantless police surveillance has led to several false arrests and accusations. For a comprehensive look at the trajectory from public lighting to ALPRs making detailed profiles of your car's movements across the country, check out this detailed and thoroughly-sourced video from one of my favorite YouTubers, Design Theory.

The Most Beautiful State: On the CDT in Wyoming

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

We crossed the border and I already felt more relaxed. The trail became more manageable, and I began to enjoy hiking again. We began to get closer to the Basin.

The Great Divide Basin is a long stretch of nothing but sagebrush for over a hundred miles. No trees in sight and barely any water along the way. Before we started our trek through the desert, we only had a day or two before we reached Rawlins, which became a haven for a double zero. Rawlins was more affordable than Colorado, and had a Pizza Hut, which JC and I discussed often during long hiking days. From there we set out.

120 miles. There were almost no natural water sources, so we had to rely on water caches. We planned our campsites around them, and timed our hikes in the early morning, late evenings, with a siesta during the hottest time of the day. JC got creative with our tent a few times for our siestas. On one particularly hot day, he threw the tent over a ditch, and we got to rest in the shade. Another time, he set it up in a way where it provided shade, but the wind blew it down on top of me.

When we were in Rawlins, I picked up my prized possession: a cheap umbrella from Walmart. I was so happy to get a little bit of shade as I was hiking, though the wind blew it around a bit.

The heat was so intense, just as it was in the desert in New Mexico. We passed sagebrush and mysterious holes, one of which JC found a black widow spider. We witnessed beautiful sunrises while listening to the worst fan fiction audiobooks we could find. The terrain, luckily, was incredibly flat, so the heat and exposure and the 28-mile stretch of no water at all were the only things we needed to worry about.

The first storm came as we had just left a solar well oasis, so we were able to turn around quickly and put up our tent near some trees. The second storm came as we decided to camp on a higher hill to enjoy the sunset. We had to quickly pack up and find a slightly lower spot and put up the tent as dark clouds descended. We huddled in the tent and waited for the storm to pass. And kept waiting. I unzipped the tent and stuck my head out. The storm that had looked so threatening had disappeared.

The last storm came on our last night in the Basin. Our last few days in the Basin were idyllic, as the temperatures decreased a bit and the clouds came in for a more overcast couple of days. It was perfect. As we were looking for a spot to camp, it seemed like there were three different storms brewing on the horizon. Luckily, the way forward didn’t have any storms ahead, so we sped up into the dark. We rushed as fast as we could, and JC found a spot with trees and a ditch to camp for the night. The storm raged on, and we were in the safest place we could possibly be.

Our trek through the Great Divide Basin ended in South Pass City, a tiny town that used to be a bustling hub during the Gold Rush. There was a store with some old-fashioned candy, where we chugged some root beer and tried candy cigarettes.

From there, we got a ride to Lander for the night. This was where we entered grizzly country, and we needed to get bear spray and an AdoTech at the outfitter. From there, we entered my favorite section of the whole trail: the Wind River Range.

So far, both New Mexico and Colorado, while very beautiful, were arid, rocky and kind of empty, with a lot of wide open spaces. The Wind River Range was lush, full of wildflowers, lakes, and trees, with sweeping mountainous landscapes. It was stunning and I could have stayed there so long. It really made me fall in love with hiking again.

JC promised to do the low routes with me, with the exception of the Cirque of the Towers. I wasn’t too sure about it, as people were talking about the descent from Texas Pass. I figured I would try the first pass on the alternate and see how it would go, as there was an alternate that would take me back to the low route. We started the alternate and began heading toward Temple Pass, which was not the best marked or maintained trail. JC and I spent a lot of time trying to find our way on the trail, and as we began to climb the switchbacks, the trail gave way to a high, eroded section that was barely trail at all. After a steep, gravelly section that seemed to go straight down, I decided to get off the alternate on my own. I was nervous to do this in grizzly country, but it would only be for half of the day. After that pass, we explored some lakes and stuck our feet in the frigid water.

The next morning, we split our different ways for the next ten miles. JC hiked on and did Jackass Pass and Texas Pass, whereas I had a calm, flat hike, through wooded alpine lakes with misty mountains against the against the overcast sky. Looking back, I am so glad that I took this alternate back to the CDT. It was one of my favorite days on trail. In the afternoon, JC and I met up again, and he talked about being underwhelmed by the Cirque of the Towers.

We hiked the rest of the Wind River section together as the trail winded through canyons and lakes. It felt like a dream, and as we exited the Wind River Range, we noticed a stark lack of lakes and wildflowers. Knowing that we were now in grizzly country made us more jumpy, especially as there were more cows making bear-like noises. We hiked through a wooded section and diverted ten miles off trail to get to the road to Pinedale.

I was so excited for Pinedale. JC and I were interested in seeing the Tetons, but we didn’t have time to do the Teton Crest Trail, so we decided to get off trail, rent a car, and spend a couple of days in Grand Teton National Park. We camped just outside of the park, and JC and I had a wonderful day, seeing the sights and doing much milder hiking. We drove around and ate snacks on Jackson Lake. It was nice to just enjoy each other’s company and be in way less life-and-death situations.

When we returned to Pinedale, we ran into NoCo, who had to get off trail due to an injury, and Leaky and Temu, who we hadn’t seen since Colorado. We also met a French hiker, Elvis, who worked hard to convince JC and I to never do the Tour du Mont Blanc in France. We spent the night at the bar, giving NoCo one last sendoff before he got off trail.

Before we knew it, we were almost done with Wyoming. The last stop before Idaho and Montana was Yellowstone National Park. I have traveled a lot, but I had never seen anything like Yellowstone. There were so many geyser basins and geothermal features. Yellowstone is one of the busiest national parks, but there were some backcountry spots where it was JC and I and the boiling, rainbow-colored pools.

As we hit Day 3 in the park, JC got the news of a family emergency back home. We got off trail for a day to figure out our options. Would JC need to fly back to the UK? It became a waiting game, and time began ticking on JC’s visa as well.

JC and I decided to keep going, knowing we may have to get off any moment due to the ongoing emergency. But we weren’t going to be able to do the whole thing. He came to an idea: we would leave Yellowstone, rent a car, and drive to Butte, MT and continue on from there. It was a dream of both of ours to see Glacier National Park, and we wanted to see it before it was impassable with snow. We took an amazing road trip through Idaho, stopping at Swig for a dirty soda.

And then we arrived in Montana, the last state of the CDT.

Hello! I'm Nikki L, or "CheeseCurd". I took a gap year to hike the Appalachian Trail and travel the world, and I rearranged my life to continue my love of thru hiking. Follow my adventure on the CDT with "JC"!

Liverpool injury news: Dominik Szoboszlai latest as 'knee' timeline assessed before Man United Premier League game at Anfield

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 09 Nov 2026 02:23, Last updated: 09 Nov 2026 02:25

Dominik Szoboszlai has suffered a knee injury and is a doubt for the international break and the games after, Liverpool boss Andoni Iraola has revealed.

The midfielder was part of the Reds team that came away from Selhurst Park with three points on Sunday, helping the Merseysiders win 3-1 against Crystal Palace.

However, Szoboszlai appeared to be struggling close to half time with a knee issue, and while he played in the second half, he was substituted in the 69th minute for Trey Nyoni.

Speaking to reporters, Iraola was not sure if Szoboszlai would play any games for Hungary over the international break, saying: "Yes, [Dominik] has, I don't know [if it's] a problem, but he definitely wasn't 100%. Let's see. The last thing we need now is to lose players in the international games.

"Last time, in the international break, we lost three very important players, so now I'm praying. It's the last thing we need. I think he extended a little bit the knee, it was fine, but not 100%, so he has to be careful to see if he can play with the national team or not.

"We will see, and also hope that it doesn't happen like the other break where we lost [Isak], Cody [Gakpo] and Dom. So, touch wood we are in a better place in two weeks."

Szoboszlai has already suffered a hamstring injury, and with Liverpool set to play Manchester United on November 22, Iraola will want the 26-year-old to rest over the next two weeks.

Knee injuries can be complex and involve lengthy periods on the sidelines, but it seems likely that Szoboszlai has avoided the worst possible outcome.

A Grade 1 problem could be a mild sprain, though that would still put the Hungarian's participation in the United game at risk given it would require anywhere from one to three weeks on the treatment table.

It would also make the 26-year-old a doubt for the club's Champions League clash with Club Brugge on November 25, though he would almost certainly be back for the Merseyside derby on November 29.

A Grade 2 injury would involve three to six weeks away from the pitch, and that could leave Szoboszlai unavailable until around Christmas.

Nyoni came on for Szoboszlai against Palace, and the 19-year-old performed excellently, winning two of his three duels and making four defensive contributions, while also playing a significant role in Alexander Isak's goal.

There is an argument that the teenager should be in the starting XI regardless of Szoboszlai's fitness, especially as he has shown he is far more comfortable with the ball in a double pivot.

The youngster was excellent against Arsenal on November 1, and if Nyoni was to play well against United at Anfield, Iraola would find it hard to drop him.

Twice in a Row, Blaney Wins Race while Larson Wins NASCAR Cup Championship

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Different Track. Different Playoff System. Same Song.

For the second year in a row, Ryan Blaney won the NASCAR Cup Series finale at the same time that Kyle Larson won the championship.

Larson cement himself as the winner to grab a championship at the end of one era and the beginning of another. Lesson's impressive playoff run after a slow start for all Chevrolet drivers brought him to the top of the charts and the table of conversation of champions. Entering Homestead, all Larson needed was to finish fourth or better in the first stage, as Joe Gibbs Racing's Chase Briscoe, Stafford, and an engine failure in stage one while Larson was running fourth ended the stage for him.

The 2024 Homestead-Miami race, while it was not the finale, created a haunting narrative for both Larson and Blaney entering this weekend. In the O'Reilly series, Saturday's race decided the title, which ultimately went to Justin Alegier for a second consecutive year; a driver ran into sand, barrels, red-flagging the final stage in a similar way that Larson did and the Cup race two years ago. Blaney lost that same race in the final laps, but found redemption by bringing home the 2026 trophy.

"I'm going to be honest with you,” Blaney said. “Every time I come here, that race (2024 Homestead loss) haunts me," said Blaney in Victory Lane. "I kind of gave that one away. I didn't do a very good job at the end of that race. Learned from it. Was able to apply some stuff we learned a couple years ago in that situation to tonight.”

Tyler Reddick, the hottest driver to start the season, finished second ahead of William Byron, third ahead of Christopher Bell, and fourth ahead of Bubba Wallace in fifth.

Larson took his championship win by finishing 12th but clenching enough points in stage one to earn the number five Hendrick Motorsports Chevrolet and his fifth ever title.

He became just the seventh racer to win three championships at the Cup level, joining names like Tony Stewart, Darrell Waltrip, Cale Yarborough, and Lee Petty, among others.

Victoria Beaver is a nomadic sports writer who spends her time hopping between race tracks and hippie farms. She’s covered every corner of motorsports that will let her in from 410 Sprints to NASCAR to Supercross. Her daily driver is a 2010 Subaru that she refused to do the smallest amount of preventative maintenance on. Instead, she spends her free time and money building a 42-foot Skoolie to one day travel the country full time.

Huawei says it’s made a 100 percent Chinese PC

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

PLUS: Japan declares infosec emergency; Apple’s makes biggest school Mac sale, ever, anywhere, in the Philippines; And more!

Huawei claims it’s built a desktop PC that uses only parts made in CHina.

The company launched the Qingyun HM650 last week and said it uses Huawei’s own Kirin X90 processor, a ten-core 20-thread machine with a clock speed of 2.3 GHz. The machine runs a newly developed enterprise version of HarmonyOS, the OS Huawei developed to end its dependence on Android and which it intends to run on most of the devices it makes.

A single Chinese government agency has reportedly signed up for 600 of the machines. That’s not an indication this machine is a curiosity, because Beijing has ordered all government entities to shift from Wintel boxes to local alternatives.

While Huawei has reportedly claimed local companies manufactured 100 percent of the components in the machine, it is unclear if all those components use Chinese designs. The Register mentions that uncertainty as many non-Chinese companies continue to operate factories in China.

Even if Huawei is using some parts that aren’t entirely Chinese, the debut of the Qingyun HM650 is bad news for Intel, AMD, and Qualcomm, as all are suffering as the PC market shrinks due to the rising cost of memory. China’s government is an enormous buyer, and losing the chance to bid for its business is unlikely to be helpful.

Japan’s government last Friday announced emergency funding to help local organizations strengthen the nation's cyber-defences.

The ¥10 billion ($63 million) package follows a wave of cyberattacks that have disrupted railways, retailers, KFC’s logistics provider, and cloud computing operators.

The emergency package aims to help Japanese government agencies to use AI to nail infosec basics, and to build tools that let the National Cybersecurity Office aggregate information about cyberattacks. Some funds will also go towards supporting victims.

Lenovo is looking for a new leader of its Asia-Pacific operations after shifting the boss, Amar Babu, to a role as mobile business group general manager and global president at Motorola.

Amar led Lenovo in the region for over five years and the company credited him with turning it into “a more balanced and diversified business and a key growth engine.”

“Across the portfolio, AP maintained its #1 PC position, while our mobile business achieved notable growth, with Motorola reaching nearly 9 percent market share in India and the #2 smartphone OEM in Japan,” according to a company announcement. “Our infrastructure business achieved profitable growth and nearly double-digit market share, while Solutions & Services continued to strengthen its best-of-breed industry position and significantly increased its advanced services business mix to over 50 percent.”

Lenovo has not named a new leader for its APAC outpost.

Vietnam’s government last week announced the nation’s first domestically-designed security chip - a 28-nanometer device that apparently integrates encryption algorithms in silicon.

The chip is already in use on secure USB storage devices that Vietnam’s government employs to store info for government and defense agencies, plus the Communist Party.

The nation hopes to use the chips to secure storage of the national digital signature certification system, and critical infrastructure systems in electricity, water, oil and gas, and telecommunications.

Hanoi says the chip needs more testing before the government will trust it to protect state secrets, but hailed the chip as an important step towards development of sovereign security capabilities.

Apple last week quietly announced it has won its biggest-ever sale of Macs into the education market in the Philippines.

Speaking on the company’s Q4 earnings call, CEO John Ternus said Apple sold 172,000 MacBook Neos to the Department of Education in the Philippines, which will deploy them to teachers and staff. ®

Mike Flanagan’s 10-Episode Netflix Thriller Masterpiece Is Everything ‘Carrie’ Should Have Been

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Carrie is missing something this show has in spades

Mike Flanagan knows how to make horror. He also knows how to make serialized TV. His most recent show is a mix of most of the things we’ve come to love about him: his bond with Stephen King, his skills as an adapter, his great eye for casting, and his ability to deliver pathos. But one key thing is missing: fear.

Carrie came out in October of this year, sparking much conversation, with many praising how it brought King’s ‘70s novel to 2026. One of its major criticisms, though, is that it’s not scary, especially for Flanagan, a guy who’s known for making some truly spooky stuff. Case in point: The Haunting of Hill House.

Released on Netflix in 2018, The Haunting of Hill House was Flanagan’s first TV show. Based on Shirley Jackson’s landmark novel of the same name, Flanagan takes her characters and reimagines them, at times scrapping their identities completely. While Jackson collected a group of strangers in a haunted house to explore their deepest traumas, Flanagan turned them into siblings, transforming Hill House into a story about their childhood home.

It’s an adaptational choice that many hardcore Jackson fans don’t forgive. Still, if Flanagan does one thing, it’s commit fully to the haunted house concept, taking the psychological fear that permeates the novel and literalizing it. Hill House is a true house of horrors.

Rather than leaning on jump scares, Hill House runs on dread, the type that has your eyes scanning through every inch of the screen so you’re not caught off guard by something that looks out of place. “If you go back and watch the series again, in every episode there’s probably somewhere between eight and 10 ghosts that are just hidden in plain sight that we called no attention to but they’re there,” Flanagan told Bloody Disgusting.

Flanagan also revealed that the ghosts had personal origins. The tall man with the bowler hat was added because one of the writers is afraid of tall people. “People on stilts freak her out,” he said. “It’s one of the most specific and weird phobias I’ve heard. We all thought it was so funny that we decided to make the tall man.”

And while Hill House delivers plenty of scary ghosts for texture and disquiet, some pull double duty, driving the story too. Flanagan splits Jackson’s Eleanor in two characters, giving her instability to the Crain siblings’ mother, Olivia (Carla Gugino), and her frailty and imagination to Nell (Victoria Pedretti). He turns the latter into the perfect example of a house cannibalizing its weakest. The series’ standout episode, “The Bent-Neck Lady,” relies on the twist that (spoiler alert) the ghost that has haunted Nell her entire life is herself, driven mad and killed by the house. Hill House’s biggest twist makes the horror intimate. Carrie’s twist does the opposite, removing the horror from her completely.

Warning: Spoilers follow for Carrie’s final episodes.

While Flanagan’s Carrie sticks closer to the book, keeping its mythology, sequence of events, and its characters' identities, it veers off in the one place it shouldn’t: the prom. We spend an entire season building up to one of the most famous moments in horror history, only for the series to unnecessarily complicate it. Instead of making the prom the place where Carrie breaks and enacts her revenge, it’s Emaline (Thalia Dudek), Carrie’s friend, who bears the majority of the blame, taking over Carrie’s body for most of the massacre.

In an interview with Polygon, Flanagan discussed the prom scene at length, clarifying what had happened. He shared that he and the writers wanted Carrie to be "100% solely responsible for" the deaths of key characters, innocent and guilty alike, including Tommy Ross (Joel Oulette), Billy Nolan (Arthur Conti) and Chris Hargensen (Alison Thornton).

They drew the line at the prom massacre. "None of us believed the Carrie White we created would consciously choose to kill Principal Grayle (Matthew Lillard), would choose to kill Ms. D (Amber Midthunder), and would choose to ignite innocent people around her,” he said.

Carrie has always been more than a story about a girl setting her prom on fire. It’s about the cruelty of high schoolers who use their weaker peers to feel better about themselves. That comes through in Flanagan’s show; as with Hill House, he has the ability to make us care deeply about his characters. But the prom is the moment where all that cruelty infects Carrie, where she transcends the page and becomes a horror icon. It’s there the show flinches. There’s no Bent-Neck Lady moment, just a massacre with no one clearly to blame. Is the show called Emaline?

The Haunting of Hill House is streaming on Netflix.

What Are The Best Seats To Book On A Widebody Long-Haul Flight?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

There is no single answer for where you should try to sit on a long-haul widebody flight because it all depends on what sort of traveler you are and what you want to prioritize. The best seat depends on whether you care most about sleep, legroom, getting off quickly, or relaxing. Whatever you prioritize, you'll have to trade off other aspects, such as convenient access to the toilet or the best window views.

Undoubtedly, the best place to sit in a widebody flight is in first or business class. Economy class varies a lot depending on where you sit, so this article focuses on that.

The first step in choosing the right seat for your economy class widebody flight is understanding how the cabin works. This is because an economy class cabin is so large on a widebody aircraft that it acts more like a set of mini-cabins, which are broken up by exit rows, galleys, toilets, and more. Some superjumbos also have an economy class enclave on the upper deck. Each mini-section of the economy class cabin is suited to different passenger archetypes.

The table below shows what you can expect in each part of the economy class cabin, making use of insights from Seat Compare:

Less busy, rapid service, helps with sleeping, and you can get off quickly

Proximity to the galley and curtain behind the premium classes

Lots of choice; often you don't have to pay too much extra for seat selection

The most activity and busiest part of the cabin will make sleeping difficult

Proximity to the galley and toilets. You may get meal service last

Rarely found due to the commonality of putting premium classes on the upper deck, and the small number of double-decker jets still flying.

Additional room, better views out of the windows, small economy class cabin

Stairs that can lead to separate boarding, and it takes longer to get off the aircraft.

In modern aviation, most airlines now expect you to pay extra to choose a specific seat. Some of the most in-demand seats, such as the exit row, cost more, though still well below premium economy or business class. As a result, an additional option opens up, particularly for solo travelers. That is to refuse to pay for seat selection and gamble that you won't get put right next to the toilet.

The most well-known path to extra legroom in economy class is to book a seat in the exit row. The extra few inches of legroom makes the seat feel closer to premium economy. It is ideal for taller passengers and often means you won't need to get up to let fellow passengers out.

Please note that these seats come with responsibility in the event of an accident. Each airline has a different minimum age to sit here, usually between 15 and 18 years old, and you also need to be physically able to operate the emergency exit.

This isn't the only reason that the exit row can be a poisoned chalice. First, aviation rules mean you can't keep any bags under the seat in front of you, as these could cause an obstruction if the aircraft must be evacuated. You'll need to be organized if you want a specific device, book, or magazine during takeoff and landing. Exit-row seats can also frustrate some passengers. These seats are fixed, often with no recline and armrests that can't be moved. The armrests also make the seat a little narrower than normal. Robustly built passengers may find this cramped. The tray tables are built into the armrest rather than on the back of the seat, which can make for an odd eating experience. For some passengers, these downsides will be irrelevant, but for others, they make exit row seats hellish.

Finding the best seat in the house can be tricky.

A poor night's sleep on a red-eye long-haul flight can steal a day from your hard-earned vacation time, making it a priority for many economy class passengers. Yet, given the inherent discomfort and crowding of economy class cabins, this can be impossible. Some seats within the class will still give you the best chance of sleep.

The first step is to secure a window seat. You'll have a wall to lean on or wedge a pillow or sweater against for additional comfort. It also means you won't be woken up when the other passengers in your row want to use the bathroom. The window seat allows you to control the blinds, though an eye mask is always a good idea.

The table below uses data collected by Simple Flying to show the window dimensions of various widebody aircraft. Pick a flight with a large window if you want to get the best views:

10.7 inches x 18.4 inches (27.2 cm x 46.7 cm)

9.5 inches x 13.5 inches (24.1 cm x 34.3 cm)

10 inches x 15 inches (25.4 cm x 38.1 cm)

9 inches x 12.3 inches (22.9 cm x 31.2 cm)

The best spots are toward the front of the economy class cabin, in front of the wings due to the engine noise, although not far forward enough to be disturbed by cabin crew clattering around in the galley. This spot, near the plane's center of gravity, also helps you avoid turbulence that can jolt you awake. Another great spot is a mini-cabin, a smaller economy class enclave formed by galleys, exit rows, and other objects that divide the cabin. These mini-cabins have fewer people, so they have less activity that could disturb your sleep.

Another popular seat that is available throughout the aircraft is the aisle seat. It has numerous benefits. Most obviously, it is more spacious than the average economy class seat, as you can enjoy some of the room in the aisle when passengers and cabin crew aren't coming past. That gives the impression of more width than the basic amount that the seat offers.

You'll find deplaning much easier, as you won't have to wait for the passenger next to you to finish before you can move. That makes the seat ideal if you have a connecting flight or need to get to your accommodation quickly. The seat also lets you access the bathroom easily, although you will also have to move whenever the other passengers in your row need to get up. Likewise, you can easily access the overhead bins, but will be disturbed when the passengers next to you need their items.

Yet, the aisle seat usually isn't ideal for passengers who want to be undisturbed or enjoy a good night's sleep. Due to the size of long-haul economy class cabins, there will constantly be other passengers and flight attendants traveling down the aisle. They could bump into you and wake you from your slumber.

Which seat should you choose for your next long-haul flight?

No seat combines everything you want to avoid on long-haul widebody flights like the final row of economy class. As you board, you may struggle to find space for your bags as the overhead bins may be partially filled with airline emergency equipment. During the flight, you'll often have no or limited recline because the seat is directly against a wall. You'll also often be close to noises and smells from the lavatory and galley. If there is a queue for the lavatory, it will always be directly next to you.

It is also common for meal services to work from the front to the back of the aircraft. Along with the wait, the best options may already be taken by the time the trolley gets to you. Finally, the rear of the aircraft is set back from its center of gravity, so you'll get the worst turbulence. The problems continue when it's time to get off the aircraft. You will often be one of the last people off the aircraft. That can cause problems if you have a tight connection. You also won't be able to get ahead of the rush to deal with immigration controls, which can cause even more delays.

The middle seat anywhere on the aircraft is also a must-avoid. You'll feel cramped between two other passengers and have no window view. This seat can become a disaster if the passengers on either side of you are large individuals. As with the window seat, you'll have to disturb the passenger next to you whenever you want to get up.

Found an error? Send it info@simpleflying.com so it can be corrected.

3 Super Growth Stocks to Buy Hand Over Fist If the Stock Market Crashes

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

One of these stocks is up 265% over the past year -- and its shares don't seem wildly overvalued, either.

Is a market crash imminent? No one knows for sure, but on the day that I'm writing this, the S&P 500 has pulled back by more than 4% -- a big move. Will that continue? We can't know. A pullback of some sort, potentially triggering a recession for a while, is likely at some point, though, because crashes and corrections simply happen every few years or so -- and several indicators are pointing to a crash.

You shouldn't keep any short-term money (funds you expect to need within the coming five, if not 10, years) in stocks, and if you're following that guideline, you should plan to just ride out any downturns. Better still, downturns present great buying opportunities. Here are some growth stocks to consider buying the next time the market corrects.

Eli Lilly (LLY -0.79%), home to popular GLP-1 weight-loss drugs such as Mounjaro and Zepbound, has been a blockbuster performer, averaging annual stock price gains of nearly 40% over the past five years. (It's up some 57% over the past year, too, as of early November.) It's no surprise, then, that the stock seems somewhat overvalued, with a recent price-to-sales (P/S) ratio of 14.7, above the five-year average of 13.3.

You could invest in it now if you plan to hold for many years, but buying after a market pullback will get you in at a lower price and boost your eventual gains.

The company is performing quite well lately, posting a 39% year-over-year increase in revenue for its third quarter while management upped its projections for the full year. It's true that its pricing has gone down a bit, but that was more than made up for by increased volume. Lilly also faces competition, but it remains a major player in the GLP-1 world and is expanding there, too, such as via its oral drug Foundayo.

Of course, Lilly is not solely a GLP-1 specialist. It's also home to Kisunla for Alzheimer's disease, Ebglyss for eczema, and cancer drug Jaypirca, among other medications.

Micron Technology (MU +5.12%) is a major semiconductor company specializing in memory and storage chips. It's been posting boffo gains lately, as it supplies memory chips used in artificial intelligence (AI) processing, and AI has been growing briskly. Micron's shares have averaged annual gains of 47% over the past decade, much of that due to outsize gains in recent years. Over the past year, for example, shares are up 265%!

Should you invest in Micron? It's certainly tempting, especially for long-term investors. The shares are even down some 26% over the past month (as of early November). That's due in large part to competition from Chinese company CXMT, as well as worries that the AI and data center build-out might take a breather. But such worries may be overblown, with rival chipmaker SK Hynix seeing a memory shortage persisting through 2030.

Meanwhile, Micron shares seem reasonably valued or a bit undervalued at recent levels, with a forward price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio of 5, below the five-year average of 11. If you hold out for a market pullback (which may happen this year but could happen later), you'll be getting in at an even more attractive point.

Then there's Nvidia (NVDA +2.77%), the semiconductor titan with a recent market value of $4.4 trillion. It actually seems attractively valued right now, with a recent forward P/E ratio of 13, well below the five-year average of 33. So you might buy into it now if you like what you see after further research.

Shares have averaged annual gains of 52% over the past 15 years, and 65% over the past three years. Growth is likely to continue, though possibly at somewhat slower rates. Nvidia is the biggest supplier for AI model training and inference, and it's expanding its scope to include software, networking, and services.

The company will be reporting its third-quarter results on Nov. 17, and Nvidia has guided for revenue to more than double year over year. If it outperforms, shares could get a nice boost, and if it doesn't, well, arguably, its shares are already down in expectation of that.

Take a closer look at any of these stocks that interest you, and as long as you plan to hold for years, you might buy some shares sooner rather than later. Otherwise, add them to your watch list.

Selena Maranjian has positions in Micron Technology, Nvidia, and SK Hynix. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Eli Lilly, Micron Technology, and Nvidia. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Core Scientific’s AI Colocation Revenue Jumps to $215M as Mining Role Shrinks

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Core Scientific reported $215.4 million in third-quarter colocation revenue, nearly 60% above the prior quarter, while billing 485 MW of capacity. Its results show how an operator once known for Bitcoin mining is building a much larger business around contracted AI infrastructure.

Some Bitcoin mining companies have talked about their artificial-intelligence ambitions for years. Core Scientific’s latest financials show what that transition looks like once facilities begin producing substantial revenue from customers.

According to its November 5 earnings release, the company generated $215.4 million of colocation revenue in the third quarter of 2026, up from $136.7 million in the second quarter and $15 million a year earlier. Total quarterly revenue reached $232.9 million.

Core Scientific said it was billing for 485 megawatts as of September 30, representing more than $700 million in average annualised colocation GAAP revenue. That is a different unit of operating performance from hashrate or Bitcoin produced. It describes contracted power and computing infrastructure delivered to tenants.

The move is important because mining revenue depends on several volatile factors at once: Bitcoin price, network difficulty, block rewards and electricity costs. Colocation can offer a different mix of long-term contracts, infrastructure obligations and construction spending. The income stream may be easier to forecast after a facility is delivered, but the capital commitment can be enormous.

Core Scientific says it is approaching full delivery under its first colocation agreement and is constructing capacity under a second agreement across five campuses. Those claims are meaningful milestones, yet remaining construction work and billing ramps still matter to the final financial outcome.

The company reported approximately $1.18 billion in quarterly net income, but said the result benefited from a non-cash change in the fair value of warrants. That makes the headline profit figure a poor stand-in for recurring cash generated by running data centres.

Adjusted EBITDA was $133.6 million, more than three times the previous quarter’s $41.1 million, while capital expenditure reached $985.1 million. Both figures deserve attention. A rapidly growing operating business can still consume substantial cash as it adds the facilities needed to earn future contracted revenue.

This is a useful counterpoint to the more familiar NewsBTC discussion of Bitcoin miners’ AI pivot. The opportunity is becoming measurable, but comparing revenue without spending, financing and delivery schedules would make the transformation look easier than it is.

Investors should follow the number of megawatts actually billing, customer diversification, operating margins, remaining capital needs and any modifications to lease terms. A nominal pipeline is less revealing than a site whose systems have been accepted by a paying tenant.

The results also offer a warning against treating every company in this sector as an identical bet on Bitcoin’s price. Core Scientific now has a material infrastructure business whose fortunes may depend more on demand for AI compute and construction execution than on daily mining profitability. Its history remains part of the story, but the metrics are changing underneath it.
